#5242. Bitcoin ETFs could surpass mutual funds in AUM by 2028: Bloomberg analyst

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

As we are about to enter the third year of the Spot Bitcoin [BTC] ETF being launched, it appears that BTC ETFs have a significantly stronger influence on BTC price returns than corporate Bitcoin accumulation.

According to the latest analysis by Ecoinometrics, an inflow of 10,000 BTC into spot Bitcoin ETFs corresponds to an estimated 3.2% boost in monthly Bitcoin returns. In comparison, the same amount of Bitcoin purchased by public companies contributes approximately 0.7% to monthly returns.

This suggests that ETF purchases have roughly 4.5 times the impact of corporate buying on Bitcoin’s price performance.

Adding more to this analysis, Bloomberg’s James Seyffart suggests that ETFs could surpass mutual funds in total Assets Under Management (AUM) within the next two to three years.

Their growth is only accelerating. If we stay on the same trend we’ve been on since the summer it will happen in ~two years.

Confirming his prediction, Syeffart used a chart and highlighted that ETFs accounted for 40.21% of total fund assets as of September 2026. And given the recent growth trends, ETFs could reach the 50% mark as early as September 2028 if the three-month growth rate continues.

However, other projections estimate this milestone between November 2028 and December 2029, depending on the period considered.

At the same time, ETFs rolling 12-month growth rate have also reached 3.74% in September 2026, above the historical average of 2.78% since the 2020 COVID market bottom. This indicates that ETF adoption is expanding faster than its long-term average.

This comes as BTC ETFs are in their outflows streak. According to Farisde Investors data, from 1st to 5th November 2026, Bitcoin ETFs saw mostly outflows. In fact, so far in November, it has already recorded $869.82 million in outflows. The only exception being on the 4th of November, when the BTC ETFs recorded a net inflow of $74.6 million.

All of this happened as the price of BTC was trading at $76,102.46 after a drop of 52% in the past 24 hours.

That said, besides Bitcoin ETFs, Ethereum [ETH] ETFs also recorded $272.09  in outflows, and Binance [BNB] ETFs recorded $271.01K in outflows. Meanwhile, XRP ETFs were funded by $529.51K in outflows, Solana [SOL] ETFs had $1.18M in outflows, and Hyperliquid [HYPE] ETFs also saw $7.66 million in outflows.

In short, the pattern suggests risk-off sentiment across crypto at the start of November, and not just in Bitcoin. So, given the current data, it remains to be seen whether Seyffart’s predictions turn into reality or not.

Ishika Kumari is a Crypto Analyst at AMBCrypto, specializing in regulatory developments, market dynamics, and blockchain’s real-world impact. She breaks down complex protocols and legislation into practical, easy-to-understand insights.

#5241. Crypto Slump Could Be Far From Over, Barclays Analyst Warns

Another sell-off could be on the way for crypto after a leading strategist at Barclays warned that the recent U.S. stock market rally may not last.

There are more signs of trouble in the cryptocurrency market, with a leading strategist at Barclays warning that the recent rebound in U.S. stocks may not last.

Alexander Altmann, the global head of equities tactical strategies at Barclays, is now somewhat skeptical of the strength of the stock market rebound after the brutal sell-off that has also dragged cryptocurrencies lower.

He lists factors such as negative market breadth, mediocre growth stock performance, a modest rebound in neocloud stocks, the ongoing crypto slump, and a flat quality factor.

The concerns from Altmann come amid increasing worries that the AI investment frenzy that has been a key driver of U.S. equity market performance may not be sustainable.

Barclays strategist was one of the few Wall Street experts who questioned whether the U.S. midterm elections would bring lasting benefits to stocks.

Barclays has flagged the risk that investment strategies sensitive to volatility could help worsen the market decline.

According to Barclays models cited in Bloomberg’s report, volatility-control funds may be at risk of dumping as much as $135 billion of U.S. equity futures in the 24 to 48 hours following Thursday’s market mayhem.

On the Oct. 29 episode of Bloomberg’s Odd Lots podcast, Altmann also argued that investors are in a “denial phase” concerning the risks to the AI sector.

He pointed to growing financing pressure on neocloud companies that host computing resources for AI applications as a sign of early stress in the industry.

#5240. SHIB Rekindles $1 Dream With Burns In Spotlight

Home » Shiba Inu (SHIB) » SHIB Rekindles $1 Dream With Burns In Spotlight

The vibrant community behind the popular memecoin just revived the $1 debate: are burns the main catalyst?

Shiba Inu’s (SHIB) official X account just quoted a rather optimistic outlook on the popular meme coin’s path forward, assessing the frequently-discussed $1 price target. Shiba Inu’s (SHIB) price tends to swing harder than the major-cap altcoins like Solana (SOL) or Ethereum (ETH) due to the speculative nature of the token.

A few days back, a crypto currency comparison between the major-caps surfaced online, and SHIB was included. In the insight, it was established that Shiba Inu (SHIB) “acts as a high-beta asset”. The analyst called it trading way below its potential, struggling due to the 589 trillion supply that makes a $1 tag “mathematically improbable, but NOT IMPOSSIBLE.”

The Shiba Inu team then shared the post with its followers and called it “nice work.” However, it’s of crucial importance to note that the Shiba Inu (SHIB) price portrayed in the graphic is overstated. The comparison table shows a $0.000012 price on the popular Japanese guard dog-themed coin. Realistically, these levels haven’t been recouped in 2026 yet.

No doubt, the big question is how to erase the 589 trillion SHIB tokens that are in circulation. At $1 each, the token’s market cap would reach about $589 trillion. That figure is simple arithmetic, price multiplied by supply, not the amount of money buyers would need to put in. Notably, this figure has considerably dropped from the initial 999 trillion due to community burns.

Presently, beyond 41% of the total supply has been torched. The regular SHIB burning by separate individuals and community initiatives has pushed SHIB’s weekly burn rate up by 190.45%, but it still revolves around millions of tokens rather than billions burned. Gradually, the stats could pick up along with the usage of Shibarium, the community’s native Layer-2 chain.

#5239. Chainlink price prediction: How LINK can avoid a breakdown below $8

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Many of the large-cap crypto assets were deep in the red amid intense selling pressure in the past 24 hours. Chainlink [LINK] was one of the assets with the most bearish sentiment in the speculative markets.

At the time of writing, the Open Interest (OI) fell 11.6% in 24 hours alongside a 7.3% price slump as the The Funding Rates also retreated toward zero but were still positive.

The past month’s liquidation heatmap revealed a notable magnetic zone at $12 and another just under $13. These clusters of liquidity could attract LINK prices higher in the short term before a move lower.

In related news, Chainlink expanded access to Asian stock markets. This tokenization push has not bred new demand for the altcoin.

The weekly chart revealed a swing level at $15 that the altcoin swept in September before falling lower. Another swing point at $10.87 was on the verge of being broken.

A weekly session close below this level would suggest a move toward $7.88, and $7 could be the next multi-week price leg.

As the liquidation heatmap showed, there was a smattering of liquidity around $10.30, which had a confluence with a recent weekly low of $10.27. Hence, a bullish short-term scenario would be a price sweep of $10.27 followed by a bounce past $11.80.

For weeks now, Chainlink has seen a steady bearish trend sustained on the 4-hour timeframe. In particular, the month-long trendline resistance (orange) was a formidable opponent for buyers.

Additionally, it could be decision time for LINK. The $10.61 swing low from mid-September was under increasing pressure from the sellers, evidenced by the lower lows in recent weeks. Moreover, something has to give, and it is likely to be the buyers’ resolve.

#5238. Samsung teams with Stripe to bring USDC to 82M phones

Samsung Pay, cards and ACH fund the account, with New York excluded and fees varying by transaction.

Eligible US Samsung Wallet users can now buy USDC with Samsung Pay, credit and debit cards, or ACH bank payments through Stripe, the payments company said Nov. 5. The service lets Galaxy owners convert dollars into stablecoins within the wallet app.

Samsung announced the feature on Oct. 7 with a rollout planned for late October, and Stripe's disclosure confirms the live purchase methods. Its cited reach of 82 million US Galaxy devices measures compatible hardware.

To add money, users open the Stablecoin Account and tap Top up, according to Samsung's support FAQ. Bank accounts and cards must be in the user's own name, and dollars added through those methods become USDC.

Users can also fund through Coinbase or receive USDC from an external wallet on a supported network. The Coinbase route opens a Coinbase webpage, where its terms and fees apply to transactions using an existing balance or an available saved payment method.

The account can hold, send, and receive USDC, while withdrawals to a bank account or debit card convert it back to dollars.

USDC stays USDC on a matching supported network. Samsung's Oct. 7, 2026 statement excludes recipient-provider charges.

Users must be at least 18 and live in a US jurisdiction where the service is available. New York residents are excluded, and each person can hold only one account in their own name.

An eligible Samsung Wallet device must run Android 13 or later and have built-in fingerprint authentication enabled. Fingerprints are required both to access the account and approve transactions.

Account opening requires identifying details including a name, address, birth date, and taxpayer identification number. Applicants may also be asked for government ID, a selfie, occupation or source-of-funds information. Stripe says its purchase flow handles identity verification, compliance and fraud prevention.

Funding, withdrawal and sending charges and processing times vary by method and destination, with transaction fees displayed before confirmation in Wallet or the provider's webpage. Banks, card issuers and external wallet providers may impose separate charges.

The October announcement said it does not charge for USDC transfers to external wallets, although recipient providers may charge.

Limits can vary by method, destination, and account status and can change over time. A maintenance fee may apply after more than a year of dormancy.

Samsung says Bastion provides the custodial wallet, with USDC held in Coinbase's institutional custody through Bastion's arrangement.

For users moving USDC to another wallet, network compatibility is a final constraint. Samsung warns that sending an unsupported asset or using the wrong network can cause permanent loss of funds.

#5237. XRP Had the Worst Day in Ages, Hyperliquid (HYPE) Awaits Volatility, What Pushes Stellar (XLM) Down? Crypto Market Review

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The cryptocurrency market remains under pressure, as most larger altcoins have corrected: large caps lost around 5% on average in the last 24 hours. Key levels have not been broken yet, but the overall trend is still bearish, despite yesterday's recovery.

From a technical standpoint, XRP has moved further into a downtrend, driven by more liquidations of long positions. According to the Nov. 5 snapshot, more than $12 million worth of longs has been liquidated. While the number itself is not critical, the long liquidations suggest that the asset saw an influx of buying, but it was quickly shut down.

The XRP Ledger activated the fixCleanup3_4_0 amendment on November 4 at 14:52 UTC. It removed an exploit that, under certain conditions, let accounts authorized to burn an issuer's tokens also mint them. The native supply of XRP has not been affected, luckily, so illicit actors have not used the exploit.

According to the technical analysis, the price is struggling to hold the important psychological threshold: $1.2 has already been broken, and XRP now trades at approximately $1.2.

The volume influx on the move toward the 21 EMA suggested a possible improvement in market conditions, but low volume on Friday, during the transition into the weekend trading session, disrupted any bullish plans.

As for the future, XRP is clearly moving in a longer-term downtrend; however, we will most likely see a local bounce toward $1.25. An EMA cross between the 100 EMA and the 50 EMA would simply be a signal of further price weakness and trend acceleration (most likely down).

The XRP Ledger has seen substantial growth in various on-chain metrics, which, unfortunately, does not give the price enough support. A lot of the Ledger activity appears to be automated, either trading bots or sophisticated LLM models. The market is mostly driven by speculative interest rather than fundamental network usage.

Hyperliquid fell by almost 9% on Nov. 5 and is now trading below $80, balancing on the 100 EMA, which has been broken temporarily. From a fundamental standpoint, Hyperliquid Strategies (PURR) disclosed that it has applied to become a U.S. futures commission merchant. If approved, it would be able to bring U.S. clients directly to the perpetual markets deployed on Hyperliquid.

In addition to this substantial regulatory milestone, Hyperliquid reported $620 million in quarterly net income, including $803 million in unrealized HYPE gains. Before that, on Oct. 29, Hyperliquid announced a separate ICE-Trade agreement; more details are available on the SEC's official website.

From a technical standpoint, on the daily HYPE/USDT chart, the price has bounced somewhat from a local psychological support level of approximately $77. It has been a recovery point for Hyperliquid, but, unfortunately, it is unlikely to hold if more selling pressure appears.

RSI, as a momentum indicator, is moving toward the 40 zone, so any surge of bearish pressure here could be short-lived if it is mostly speculative. However, with volume rising during the descending local trend, the bearish trend is being reinforced.

Hyperliquid (HYPE) lost its pace by the end of September, and a lower high formed in the first days of October, which is essentially the market's main signal of the end of the rally. Despite the spike at the beginning of November, it is still a lower high, which means the bearish trend is not going anywhere.

This is a relatively hard prediction to make, considering the unpredictable nature of the market. Unfortunately, the price has barely any support left, and the next support is the 200 EMA (black line on the chart), which coincides with the $68 price level.

Volume, on the other hand, is falling, which is either a normal tendency before the weekend trading session or a sign of a weakening trend for now.

Key price levels for HYPE are: $80 as the closest psychological support level, $68 as the 200 EMA, and $89 as the first psychological resistance level. The trendline resistance is at approximately $84, so a breakthrough there should become a foundation for a price recovery.

After Stellar (XLM) fell toward $0.157, there is a good chance that we are seeing accumulation or at least a transition into a sideways trend. The moving averages are not converging, which signals trend acceleration, and in this case it is a bearish one. Whenever moving averages converge, volatility is more likely to spike.

The trend on Stellar is accelerating, while volume remains relatively low and neutral.

It is no coincidence that XLM saw a trend slowdown at approximately $0.15, as it coincides with the point where the previous trend started. The death cross that occurred at the end of October is also a bearish signal.

It was followed by a spike in trading volume, which confirms the significance of the indicator and suggests that the trend may continue.

The latest network milestone for XLM is the release of Bell (Protocol 30), which was set to go live on testnet on November 3. The key change it brought to the network was post-quantum signature verification for smart contracts and millisecond ledger timestamps.

However, the mainnet validator vote, which will make the update available to users on the live network, is set to take place on November 23 at 17:00 UTC.

The bearish price performance on the market was followed by a recorded 500,000 XLM long liquidation. But considering the sheer volume of XLM on exchanges, a sub-1 million liquidation barely affects the price and will most likely have a very minor effect on the market.

From the momentum standpoint, RSI is already at a local bottom, and a further drop is possible, despite RSI already sliding to around 30. An oversold RSI is not really an issue when there is profitable capital present.

Considering the behavior of the market, there is a good chance that most of the buying support was at 1.5-1.6, so a break below the $1.5 support level could trigger a further decline, with a possible acceleration toward $1, though this is unlikely and possible only in a very bearish market.

#5236. RealFi Nears Ethereum Launch, Weighs XRPL Expansion

Home » Trendsetter News » RealFi Nears Ethereum Launch, Weighs XRPL Expansion

RealFi is targeting a December 1 Ethereum mainnet launch while exploring a possible XRP Ledger expansion.

RealFi plans to launch on Ethereum mainnet on December 1, according to a Cardano-focused host reviewing the project’s expansion plans. The Angry Crypto Show puts the move at the center of a broader question: whether Cardano-originated financial products can attract users and liquidity beyond their home network.

The host cites Token Terminal data showing RealFi has surpassed $121.7 million in asset transfer volume on Cardano. That figure measures transfers, not deposits or total value locked. The transcript does not specify the year of the planned Ethereum launch.

In remarks quoted from an interview with David Gokhshtein, RealFi CEO John O’Connor argues that stablecoins keep growing because they serve a practical purpose: moving stable-value money across borders. He identifies yield-bearing products as the fastest-growing part of that business.

An XRP Ledger expansion is less definite. O’Connor points to the network’s high fully diluted valuation and relatively limited DeFi activity as reasons to explore an integration.

“That’s a conversation we’re kicking off to see whether there’s value for us to come over there,” he says, according to the transcript. No launch date, partnership agreement or technical implementation is announced.

The host sees a potential exchange: Cardano-originated products could gain access to XRP users and liquidity, while offering additional financial functionality. That is the commentator’s investment thesis, not evidence of committed demand.

The video also reports that more than $450,000 worth of bitcoin had been supplied to Cardano’s Strike Finance within eight days of its integration of a bitcoin collateral asset. The figure offers another adoption signal, though the transcript provides no longer-term comparison.

Whether RealFi delivers its announced Ethereum launch, secures integrations and attracts sustained usage. An XRP Ledger expansion could broaden distribution, but O’Connor’s qualification matters – the project wants wider access only where the numbers justify it.

#5235. XRP Price Trapped at $1.1799 After $30 Million Cascade Liquidation

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

XRP’s leveraged market went through a series of brutal flushes this week. In just five days, derivative traders who bet on the token’s upside have lost more than $30 million, according to CoinGlass. What happened is a sudden price reversal after a moment of optimism on November 4 that caught buyers off guard and set off a cascade of liquidations all across the crypto board.

Liquidations hit extreme levels by Friday, running 2.74 times above the weekly average. Exchanges' data shows a completely lopsided picture where longs account for 95.54% of all daily losses worth $16.01 million out of a total $16.75 million. Shorts got off with a scare, losing a token $746,000. Of course, Binance as the most liquid platform was the place of the most pain, with $8.06 million in leveraged positions on XRP wiped out on the exchange.

What played out is a textbook long squeeze. Traders opened overleveraged positions hoping the rally would continue, got trapped in a falling trend, and their forced closures started knocking each other out down the chain. More than 3,600 traders worldwide lost their deposits completely.

XRP dropped 6.41% over the past days on the spot market, leaving the price pinned at $1.1799 by the weekend. How strong sellers are is evident by the XRP/USD chart on TradingView. Here the weekly candle comes to closure with a long upper wick, meaning every bullish attempt to take control and buy the dip above $1.22 was shut down.

For XRP buyers, the technical picture looks worrying:

The price has settled below key moving averages, the 50 EMA and 100 EMA. The latter has now turned into hard resistance at $1.271.

The Relative Strength Indicator is at 33.21 with the 'bearish' divergence appearing earlier on the chart fully playing out, and the trend is clearly pointing down.

The next dangerous trigger for the market is at $1.1588 as that’s where the nearest “long pain” zone lies, according to Coinglass. The price is just 1.79% away from that line.

Algorithms put the odds of the chart testing this level within the next 24 hours at roughly 50%. If bulls give up this line too, a liquidation cascade will force-close another 928,900 in positions and speed up the decline. Buyers right now have no support above current XRP price, while sell pressure below is estimated at under half a million of dollars.

#5234. Tom Lee says Ethereum is bottoming – A whale is betting $45M that he’s right

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Volatility often goes hand in hand with speculation, and Ethereum is a clear example of this.

The past 48 hours have been a rollercoaster for the altcoin. According to CoinGlass, nearly $300 million in long positions have been wiped out.

However, this still does not compare to the liquidity squeeze seen earlier in October, which saw almost $500 million of positions liquidated as ETH broke below the $2.5k support level, triggering a 7% correction.

This time, despite erasing nearly $300 million worth of long positions, ETH has corrected by just over 1%. This suggests that selling pressure remains relatively contained, at least for now. Could this mean Tom Lee was right to say Ethereum is close to bottoming out?

As can be seen in the post above, a trader who claims to have a perfect 100% win rate has opened up another long trade on Ethereum. The trader had used 25x leverage to go long on 20,277 ETH for an investment of $45.3 million.

This trade has already resulted in $252,000 of profit. The interesting part is that the trader’s  previous 10 trades have all been profitable, resulting in $6.32 million in realized gains.

Given this track record and the fact that ETH has had limited downside despite heavy futures selling, this could be an interesting sign.

It could suggest that the trader is positioning ahead of a potential recovery that the broader market has yet to price in, adding another bullish angle to Tom Lee’s $2k ETH bottom thesis.

That said, the real test may still be ahead.

The market is still not fully convinced that Ethereum [ETH] has found its bottom.

For starters, on-chain data suggests that uncertainty is also showing up in staking demand, which continues to weaken. According to ValidatorQueue, there are about 1,036,882 ETH (over $2.3 billion) waiting to go into the Ethereum mainnet validator queue.

This is a considerable drop compared to 3,737,802 ETH six months ago, suggesting fewer investors are looking to lock up their ETH for staking rewards.

Meanwhile, institutional sentiment remains negative. Over the past five weeks, there have been continuous outflows from Ethereum ETFs, with $270 million in net outflows already recorded in less than a week since November began.

If this trend continues, November could see outflows exceed October’s $1.6 billion, putting further pressure on ETH and its recovery prospects.

Unless these factors start to improve, calling an Ethereum bottom could be premature.

For now, ETH remains exposed to further downside. Long positions are building despite fading technicals, weakening interest in staking demand, and increased ETF outflows.

On top of this, a large long-liquidation cluster exists below $2200 ETH. If overall sentiment remains weak, ETH could sweep this liquidity, potentially falling below the $2k level before a potential reversal.

This suggests that the market may need another round of downside before Tom Lee’s bottom thesis gets stronger support.

A big Ethereum trader is betting on a recovery despite recent market volatility.

Falling staking demand, ETF outflows, and liquidation risks could push ETH below $2,000.

#5233. Zero-click iOS exploits drain $4M USDT from active TRON wallets

The 365-address TRON network includes 89 TRX-only losses, while just one theft matches a published iPhone-owner account.

Blockchain data firm Bitquery has traced at least $4 million in USDT thefts to a network involving 365 TRON addresses.

Its Nov. 5 investigation adds a loss figure to recent iPhone wallet-theft reporting, while linking just one counted theft to a published iPhone-owner account.

The investigation covers Oct. 5 through Nov. 4, and the 365-address total includes 89 addresses that lost only TRX.

Bitquery linked 43 collector wallets through funding flows to a common wallet used to pay transaction fees. Those financial links establish a network, with common control by a single operator remaining unproven.

#5232. SWIFT Debuts Own XRP-Inclusive Ledger With Dozen Banks

Home » Ripple (XRP) » SWIFT Debuts Own XRP-Inclusive Ledger With Dozen Banks

Ripple’s XRP extends connections to multiple banks in SWIFT’s blockchain-adopting line-up.

The biggest European financial conglomerate’s grandiose move to release their own blockchain ledger didn’t go unnoticed. This November as planned, the Society for Worldwide Interbank Financial Telecommunication (SWIFT) debuted the pilot version with 12 banks, some of which are familiar names in the XRP holder community.

One of them, HSBC, is slated to start blockchain-powered operations. HSBC connects its own Tokenised Deposit Service (TDS) to SWIFT’s ledger. The major British bank actively employs Metaco’s Harmonize platform for institutional digital-asset custody (Metaco was acquired by Ripple in 2023). The enterprise-level acquisition strengthened that bond.

5 OF SWIFT'S 17 LEDGER BANKS ALREADY WORK WITH RIPPLE 🚨Swift's new blockchain ledger is live with 17 banks piloting 24/7 cross-border payments.Look who's on that list:🏦 HSBC🏦 Standard Chartered🏦 DBS🏦 FirstRand🏦 BNY, which holds RLUSD's reserves and now holds XRP… https://t.co/z7UOp1KBYz pic.twitter.com/nNP5Ql4vgn

The bank also has historical references to XRPL capabilities in internal materials and some past explorations, with an agenda to expand blockchain-powered business operations. Conducting business operations on Distributed Ledger Technology (DLT) based chains like Ripple’s XRP Ledger serves an advantage of instant settlement & cost-effectiveness.

Aside from them, there’s a few more in the 12-long list. Standard Chartered, another popular British bank, has been running the XRP-related tech stack since 2016. During October 2026 the traditional finance behemoth launched their institutional custody for Ripple’s RLUSD stablecoin on the XRP Ledger through their Dubai (DIFC) branch.

Also, there’s DBS and BNY (BNY Mellon). These two Ripple-tied major banks serve completely different purposes. BNY Mellon is the primary reserve custodian for RLUSD. Meanwhile, DBS is partnered with Franklin Templeton to list a tokenized money-market fund (sgBENJI) on XRP Ledger, tradeable against RLUSD on their digital exchange.

🚨 ES OFICIALSWIFT acaba de confirmar transacciones en vivo en blockchain con más de 12 bancos.Y los reportes dicen que está impulsado por $XRP.Este es el tipo de noticia que la gente lleva años esperando.El sistema antiguo finalmente está siendo reemplazado en tiempo real?… pic.twitter.com/2uJi0VGPI9

This is a distinct product from the SWIFT project. SWIFT’s blockchain ledger is compatible with multiple DLT networks, but Ripple’s XRP Ledger has a crystal-clear advantage in regulatory compliance and native traits. XRP, along with Stellar’s XLM & Hedera’s HBAR has native ISO 20022 support, the new gold standard in financial messaging.

The cross-border payments market, worth over $155 trillion in processed volume annually, powers global trade, remittances & commerce. Yet merchants admit it remains slow, costly and not too efficient when compared to blockchain-based counterparts. 70% of merchants expect all transactions to settle immediately by 2027, which aligns with SWIFT’s vision.

However, Ripple’s business ventures don’t always translate into blockchain adoption, as seen in the 2025 example. Ripple Prime, the custody arm, processed over $13 trillion without the help of crypto.

#5230. MIT develops robot that slithers through intestines like a snake

Published Oct 29, 2026 3:10 PM EDT

Worms are usually the last thing someone wants in their gut. Just ask this unfortunate Alaskan bear recently spotted with several multi-foot long tapeworms hanging out of its butt. However, a  new type of gut-crawling worm being designed by engineers at the Massachusetts Institute of Technology (MIT), may actually be here to help.

Their soft robot, called BIOSENTER, is designed to autonomously inch through the crowded, cramped, and coiled small intestine, contracting and extending its body much like an earthworm or snake. It does that by alternately pumping air through four inflatable chambers on either side of its hollow body. Once inside, the wiggly worm-bot can snap photos, administer drugs, and even take small biopsy samples. It can also slither into the intestine via a person’s mouth or rear-end all by itself.

Horrifying as all of this might sound, the team behind it says the robot could offer a faster, less labor-intensive alternative to double-balloon enteroscopy, the current standard for examining  the small intestine. And with intestinal diseases and cancers on the rise, any methods to detect issues early could have potentially life-saving results. The team published details about the robot this week in the journal Science Robotics.

The small intestine is a particularly challenging place to run diagnoses. Though it can measure 20 feet long, it’s tightly coiled up in a cramped part of the abdomen. It is also hemmed in by the stomach and colon, which can make it difficult to reach.  But bleeding, lessons, and blockages can develop in the small intestine which can lead to big problems if left untreated. Currently, the most common way to examine the organ is double-balloon enteroscopy. Healthcare workers repeatedly inflate and deflate two balloons to work a scope through the digestive tract. The method works, but it’s slow and requires constant hands-on attention.

“The fundamental challenge that we’re trying to overcome is how to evaluate that 20-to-25-foot segment of the small intestine in a way that can be more automated,” Giovanni Traverso, an MIT mechanical engineer and  study-co-author, said in a statement.

The team turned to nature for a solution: snakes. These scaly reptiles move by alternating contracting muscles on either side of their body. Aside from offering protection, their scaly bodies also give them extra traction as they slither. The MIT researchers looked at this natural byproduct of evolution and saw a design that could work well in the narrow, slippery corridors of the human GI tract.

With the snake inspiration in mind, the team built a soft, rubbery robot with a hollow body. Its worm-like frame measures about 3.7 inches in length with a half inch diameter. These dimensions make it compact enough to travel through the small intestine, as well as other narrow passages in the body, like the esophagus. BIOSENTER has four inflatable chambers arranged along either side of its tubular body.  These chambers inflate and deflate in alternating combinations, which is how it moves forewarn and bends to change directions. Keeping with the snake theme, the robot’s exterior is fitted with a textured, scale-like coating that improves its traction.

Additionally, the worm has expandable sections built in its head and tail inspired by a type of Japanese origami called kirigami, that uses cuts as well as folds. These inflatable segments alternately grip and release the intestinal wall, anchoring the robot so it can pull itself forward. BIOSENTER’s hollowed out body houses a variety of tools: cameras for imaging, a sprayer for drug delivery, and tiny clippers to take tissue samples. The entire thing is tethered to an external system via cable. Though it’s meant to move mostly autonomously, healthcare workers can also control BIOSENTER through a handheld device reminiscent of an Xbox controller.

“By using kirigami sleeves to enhance surface friction, the robot can generate localized tissue engagement traction for locomotion without relying on excessive stretching of the delicate small intestinal tissue,” added MIT graduate student Ahmad Mujtaba Jebran..

The team tested the robot on several sedated,  living pigs, because their GI tract anatomy resembles the kind humans have. During testing, BIOSENTER navigated the pigs’ intestines for 11 minutes, capturing real-time images, collecting small tissue biopsies, and delivering fluid to specific locations.

BIOSENTER notably isn’t the first gut-crawling robot. In 2021, a team from the University of Macau in China designed a prototype robot inspired by an African spider that moves using a cartwheel motion. In that case, the robot is injected via a vitamin sized capsule and traverses the GI tract (including the small intestine) via a magnetic field. Rather unglamorously, the spider bot is expelled by pooping.

#5228. Moscow Exchange sets December 1 to launch Bitcoin and Ethereum trading

Russia's final Bitcoin, Ethereum, and USDT rules take effect Nov. 17, with retail purchase limits and testing.

The Bank of Russia has finalized the list of assets that investors without qualified status can buy, allowing exchanges to list Bitcoin, Ethereum, and USDT for public trading from Nov. 17.

In its Nov. 6 announcement, the central bank said non-qualified investors can buy through brokers, asset managers, or crypto exchangers, on exchanges, or over the counter. Purchases will be limited to 300,000 rubles annually through each intermediary, and investors must undergo testing before a transaction.

The directive defines the calculation as the ruble sum of acquisition costs for digital currencies already bought and being bought through that broker during the calendar year.

The broker calculation is tied to the calendar year, and both completed purchases and orders under consideration enter the sum. The broker applies the ceiling to those combined acquisition costs during the calendar year, so earlier purchases through that broker remain part of the calculation when another order is assessed.

Before transacting, investors must undergo the test and familiarize themselves with crypto investment risks regardless of whether they hold qualified status.

Qualified investors will be able to access any cryptoassets without the non-qualified investor asset-list and purchase-limit restrictions. They must also undergo testing and familiarize themselves with the risks.

The central bank said it selected liquid assets using market capitalization, average daily trading volume, and a foreign-market pricing history of at least five years. Those criteria explain the restricted list available through the non-qualified investor route.

#5225. EIP-8037 state-gas rules force Ethereum dApps to rewrite billing

A Nov. 6 appeal asks projects to test gas assumptions and report repair options that could shape Glamsterdam’s specifications.

Ethereum’s core developers are asking application teams to help decide whether proposed gas changes in the Glamsterdam upgrade need revision, because their own audits cannot cover every project’s contracts and off-chain services.

In a Nov. 6 post on the Ethereum Foundation blog, Marius van der Wijden asked developers and auditors to check eight gas assumptions affected by the proposed upgrade. Security auditors ChainSecurity and Dedaub are investigating alongside internal audits, but the review also needs projects to explain how they could repair affected systems.

The Hoodi testnet’s planned Oct. 26 activation and accompanying 200 million gas-limit increase were postponed on Oct. 22.

The Nov. 6 appeal says the delay continues pending resolution, while the notices leave mainnet activation unset.

The consequences extend from relayers recovering too little of their transaction costs to wallet authorizations becoming reusable after a particular kind of failure. That makes application repairability part of the evidence developers need when weighing changes to the upgrade.

Glamsterdam’s EIP-8037 would raise the cost of creating persistent blockchain data and meter that state growth separately from execution, and EIP-8038 would reprice access and writes to existing state.

The aim is to support higher throughput while controlling the data Ethereum nodes must retain.

The eight-invariant framework separates three price assumptions, covering fixed budgets and reserves, from five accounting assumptions involving gas returns, failure handling, billing and delegation.

A relayer pays to execute a transaction for someone else and may recover that expense by measuring gas before and after the work. The framework’s INV-5 entry explains how the proposed accounting can disrupt that calculation.

Under EIP-8037, a transaction initially funds a separate state-gas reservoir when its gas limit exceeds 16,777,216. State creation draws from that pool first, then execution gas. Solidity’s gasleft() reading excludes the reservoir, so subtracting two readings can miss costs that the relayer actually pays.

If the later reading exceeds the earlier one, checked subtraction reverts, blocking the operation. Unchecked subtraction can wrap into a large number and potentially produce excessive reimbursement, depending on the surrounding arithmetic and payment checks.

Meanwhile, invisible reservoir spending can leave users undercharged and operators covering the shortfall.

The billing guidance suggests agreeing on a user-signed fee using an estimate under the new rules. Where metering remains necessary, clamping the subtraction and capping payment at a signed maximum limits the charge, while reservoir-funded costs remain outside the measurement.

An open specification proposal, PR #12581, would prevent gas readings from increasing. That would address the subtraction problem, but the reservoir’s billing blind spot would remain.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Under the proposed Glamsterdam rules, gasleft() excludes the state-gas reservoir. Select a funding state to see how the balance charged for one cold new-slot write changes.

Illustrative state with at least 97,920 gas in the reservoir at this write. The execution balance pays 12,100 and the reservoir pays 97,920.

Illustrative state with zero reservoir gas at this write. The execution balance pays the complete 110,020 charge, including the state component.

A funded reservoir can pay the state charge outside gasleft(), while an empty reservoir shifts that charge to the execution balance.

Proposed rules checked November 6, 2026. Operation-level charges exclude intrinsic, wrapper and incidental instruction costs. Initial reservoir funding requires a transaction gas limit above 16,777,216, and later refills may also fund it. Intermediate reservoir balances split the state charge between balances.

Uses the proposed charge for one cold zero-to-nonzero storage write. 12,100 execution + 97,920 state = 110,020 total gas. Funding illustrations assume at least 97,920 reservoir gas or zero at that write. The main bar is proportional, rounded to 0.001 SVG units. Connectors are schematic.

Proposed rules checked November 6, 2026. Operation-level charges exclude intrinsic, wrapper and incidental instruction costs. Initial reservoir funding requires a transaction gas limit above 16,777,216, and later refills may also fund it. Intermediate reservoir balances split the state charge between balances.

INV-8 examines EIP-7702 delegation, where an account authorizes delegated contract code. Under the proposed rules, insufficient gas during pre-execution can leave a transaction included in a block while rolling back its delegation and authorization-related update to the account’s sequence number, or nonce.

An exposed authorization that still satisfies its validity checks, including the authorizing account’s nonce, can then be reused. If the delegated contract also has an initializer that lets the first caller choose the owner, an attacker could apply the authorization and initialize ownership in one transaction.

Authorization front-running already existed in public mempools, but here a failed mined transaction can expose a reusable authorization even after private submission.

Delegations already applied before the top-level execution frame remain in place when that later frame fails. A failed receipt requires further checking of the account’s resulting code and nonce.

Suggested wallet safeguards include binding initialization to the account’s signature or self-calls and invalidating exposed authorizations. Charging the worst-case authorization cost upfront has also been suggested as a protocol change and remains unadopted.

Under the proposed schedule, creating a cold storage slot costs 110,020 total gas, versus 22,100 under Osaka. Of that proposed total, 12,100 is execution gas, and 97,920 is state gas, with the reservoir determining which balance funds the latter.

ChainSecurity’s Oct. 13 research shows how changed costs and returned gas can let a contract finish recording an underfunded operation as permanently unsuccessful. If that record removes the retry path, a valid operation can become impossible to repeat.

Revalidated budgets, propagating failures, and preserving retries address different parts of that risk.

The repository asks affected projects whether a setting, upgrade, or migration can fix their problem, and roughly when. Those answers help developers assess how much compatibility work applications can absorb and where specification changes could prevent damage.

Concrete reports of what breaks and how to repair it will inform the next specification discussions.

#5224. Argentine Bitcoiners Debut “The Genesis of Satoshi Nakamoto” Artwork in Downtown Buenos Aires

“The Genesis of Satoshi Nakamoto,” a 2.4-meter, multi-dimensional cube, was presented on October 29 at Polo Dot as part of the Open Fest of LABITCONF 2026. Driven by Bitcoin.ar through the Premio B·Arte, the work combines art, technology, and education in a journey through Bitcoin’s fundamental principles. The inauguration featured guided remarks by Rodolfo Andragnes and a musical performance by Mayumaná, according to an announcement by the organization.

Buenos Aires now has a monument dedicated to Satoshi Nakamoto, the person—or group of people—behind the creation of Bitcoin, whose identity remains unknown.

Driven by Bitcoin.ar through the fifth edition of the Premio B·Arte, the monument was conceived not only as a tribute to Satoshi, but also as an artistic and educational experience. Through its five faces—four lateral and one upper—it proposes a journey to approach different concepts that explain Bitcoin’s origin, functioning, and philosophy.

During the inauguration, Rodolfo Andragnes — co-founder of Bitcoin.ar and LABITCONF — accompanied the public with words and explanations about the details of the work, guiding the reading of its various elements and the meaning behind each representation. The presentation was accompanied by a musical show by Mayumaná, which added rhythm and sound to the opening of this new space dedicated to Bitcoin’s history.

Each of the monument’s faces offers a gateway to a different aspect of Bitcoin. The figure of Satoshi Nakamoto, for example, represents the anonymous character of its creator and the collective dimension of a technology that, once set in motion, continued to grow thanks to thousands of people distributed around the world. The reflections and multiplied geometric forms also evoke the structure of a network that replicates and expands.

Another of its faces addresses Bitcoin’s programmed scarcity through a cube that successively divides into halves in a Fibonacci sequence. The representation refers to “the halving”, the mechanism that approximately every four years halves the reward miners receive for validating blocks and that regulates the issuance of new bitcoins. Starting at 50 bitcoins per block, the total reward gets cut in half every four years for roughly 130 years, until 21 million bitcoins have been emitted and the block reward becomes zero. This supply limit is iconic to the cryptocurrency and key to its qualities as hard money.

The work also incorporates the first words of Bitcoin’s foundational document, the white paper published by Satoshi Nakamoto in October 31st,  2008. There, the possibility of making electronic payments directly between people, without needing to go through a financial institution, is proposed. That idea constitutes a foundational principle of the technology.

Another face reproduces information from the genesis block, the first block of the Bitcoin chain, created on January 3, 2009. Among its elements appears the reference to the headline from The Times newspaper that Satoshi incorporated into that block, a historical mark that places Bitcoin’s birth in its context and anchors it to history and time.

Thus, the monument proposes something more than contemplating a sculpture: it invites one to walk around it, observe it from different angles, and discover the concepts behind each of its parts; “The intention is to bring Bitcoin closer to those who are already part of its community, but also to awaken the curiosity of those who do not yet know its history.”

The monument was created by Ricardo Alonso, engineer, and Celeste Difabio Videla, architect, a couple of artists from Bariloche who won the fifth edition of the Premio B·Arte. The work measures 2.40 meters in width, length, and height, and is made of stainless steel and glass.

Its construction involved teams and collaborators from different localities across the country, in a process that combined different trades, materials, and techniques. Aída Pippo, visual artist, researcher of new technologies applied to art, and member of the Board of Directors of Bitcoin.ar, led the project and coordinated the different stages of production and assembly.

“It gives me great pride to feel that this is something we can leave in our city and in our country,” Andragnes expressed during the presentation, highlighting the value of a collective initiative that seeks to leave a cultural and educational footprint.

For their part, the creators noted that they sought for the work to function as a meeting point for the community and, at the same time, as a gateway for those who do not yet know Bitcoin. “You don’t always have the possibility of leaving something for posterity,” they shared when speaking of the experience of seeing the monument finished and installed in Buenos Aires.

With this inauguration, Bitcoin.ar seeks for the monument to become a permanent resource for the city: a space that schools, universities, families, and visitors can approach to learn Bitcoin’s history, discover its principles, and open new conversations about the future of money and technology. The intention is that what is presented today as an artistic work can continue to grow through educational activities and new experiences, and remain in Buenos Aires as a legacy for future generations.

The project was financed by the Fundación Bitcoin Iberoamérica and represents a new way of bringing Bitcoin’s history and principles to public and cultural spaces, beyond screens and specialized environments.

#5223. Millions watched Kalshi’s election odds without betting a dollar

On election night, millions watched Kalshi's probabilities shift in real time, even though most visitors never traded a contract.

Kalshi says people watching its midterm markets outnumbered traders five to one. For those spectators, Kalshi offers a forecast that keeps moving while the country waits for an answer.

You could spend election night on Kalshi without buying anything, as millions of people did this week. Its midterm display offered maps of congressional races and gauges tracking control of the House and Senate, with percentages changing in real time as the evening unfolded.

It's easy to understand why traders keep such a close eye on Kalshi: every little change in odds changes the value of their positions. Given how much money actually moved hands during the midterms, it's no wonder hundreds of thousands of traders were glued to the platforms.

But millions of people were also looking at Kalshi who weren't making any bets, with most not even having accounts on the platform. In October, Kalshi said viewers of its midterm markets outnumbered traders five to one.

Kalshi’s post-election recap reported more than $2.5 billion in midterm trading volume, compared with $1.3 billion for the 2024 election cycle.

Kalshi's Nov. 4, 2026 recap reports $1.3 billion for the 2024 election cycle and more than $2.5 billion for 2026 midterm markets. The 2026 bar marks the reported lower-bound threshold, not an exact total. Kalshi uses notional volume to measure trading activity; these figures do not count viewers or unique traders. The recap does not specify matching market coverage or start and end times for the two periods.

Source: Kalshi: 2026 midterms recap, Nov. 4, 2026 · Kalshi: prediction-market volume convention, footnote 1

While Kalshi's numbers are hard to verify and the company hasn't provided an exact measurement period or a definition of its ratio, they show a second use for the exchange: following politics without placing a bet, while letting the people who do bet help shape your expectations.

On Aug. 20, 2008, Wolf Blitzer turned to CNN's internet reporter, Abbi Tatton, for an update on speculation about Barack Obama's running mate. Tatton brought him a number from Intrade, an online prediction market: Joe Biden was trading at about 36%.

Biden led the other candidates she named and would ultimately get the job, although 36% still left substantially more room for someone else to be chosen.

Tatton also mentioned CNN's own political market, which used virtual dollars. Viewers could follow the odds or play along without risking real money. Everybody wanted to put a number on political suspense, and it was a perfect fit for a television segment about the elections.

Kalshi's election hub combines market odds with polling averages, fundraising figures, and campaign news, giving visitors several ways to follow a race in one place.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

A contract paying $1 if a candidate wins, and nothing if they lose, might trade at 65 cents. That price is commonly read as roughly a 65% chance of victory, although fees, available trading volume, and the contract's rules slightly erode those numbers.

Polls gather answers from a sample of people, forecasts estimate an outcome based on historical evidence and assumptions, and a market then sets a price at which participants will trade. These can inform one another, but a percentage alone doesn't reveal how it was produced or what it measures.

The trader has to decide whether 65 cents is worth paying, and the spectator can simply take away the expectation that the candidate will probably win. That means that someone else's financial judgment has become part of their understanding of the election.

The market still has something to show you between a poll's release and the next substantial development. It can adjust after a debate, absorb new returns, or reflect a change in how traders interpret already available evidence.

Those movements look much the same on a screen: a percentage rises or falls, inviting the viewer to read the change as news.

Kalshi acknowledges in its explanation of why prices move that fresh information is only one cause. Trader behavior can also push prices away from a reasonable estimate, and watching the percentage doesn't necessarily reveal what has happened.

A candidate can appear to be gaining ground because the chance attached to their name has increased, even when no additional voter has been interviewed and no additional ballot counted.

An experiment involving 1,994 registered voters showed how easily those percentages can shape expectations.

Participants saw pairs of hypothetical congressional candidates, with randomly varied polling figures and market odds. Higher displayed odds made respondents more likely to identify a candidate as the expected winner.

Traders can bring knowledge that a casual observer lacks, and money gives someone who spots a mistaken price a reason to act. The commercial effort to attract an audience doesn't remove the possibility that the resulting forecast is good.

But a poll and a market reacting to that poll can look like two separate endorsements of a candidate's prospects when they draw on the same evidence. Those just observing the market get more perspectives without necessarily getting more independent information.

In its post-election recap, Kalshi said its markets reached 99% for Democratic House control at 8:10 p.m. Eastern and passed that level for Senate control at 9 p.m.

The company said its prices reflected anticipated results ahead of television networks and offered replays showing how the graphics changed through the evening.

But an exchange and a newsroom have to answer different questions, since a market can express overwhelming confidence while leaving room for a loss.

The Associated Press says it calls a race only when the trailing candidates no longer have a path to victory. For congressional control, it counts the seats actually needed for a majority.

Neither a price nor a news organization's call is the official certification of an election. But the call takes responsibility for saying the outcome has been established, a different claim from assigning it a probability.

A market that gets to 99% first doesn't show that journalists took longer to perform the same task.

AP supplies Kalshi with vote counts and race calls, which the exchange places alongside its forecasts and traders can use to reconsider their positions. The market depends in part on the reporting against which it is judged.

Kalshi's recap points readers toward international elections and the 2028 presidential contest, inviting them to follow the odds beyond one election night.

Spectators will most likely want an early estimate from a prediction market but still wait for a newsroom to declare a winner.

The price of these contracts shows how strongly traders expect an outcome, and the race call says the remaining votes can no longer change it. People who never risk a dollar still have to decide how much authority to give the price, especially when its movement is the only thing that has changed.

Armed with a classical education and an eye for news, Andjela dove head deep into the crypto industry in 2018 after spending years covering politics.

#5222. South Korean Crypto Exchange Gopax Suspends Withdrawals and Trading After Hacking Threat

South Korea's Gopax halted standard withdrawals at 2:55 p.m. and trading at 3:53 p.m. Friday after a hacking-threat email, and is transferring all held assets to cold wallets with no loss reported so far.

South Korean cryptocurrency exchange Gopax suspended withdrawals and trading on Friday after receiving a hacking-threat email, and said it is moving all held virtual assets into offline cold wallets as a preemptive step to protect customer funds.

Gopax halted standard deposit and withdrawal services at 2:55 p.m. local time and stopped trading at 3:53 p.m., Yonhap News Agency reported, citing the exchange. Direct withdrawals through the website and app are unavailable. Customers who want coins out must apply through the customer support center, where requests are processed manually after internal verification.

“To ensure the safe custody of user assets, we plan to transfer all virtual assets to cold wallets for storage,” Gopax said in remarks carried by Seoul Economic Daily. “Direct withdrawals through the Gopax website or app are temporarily unavailable, and customers wishing to withdraw funds should apply through the Gopax customer support center.”

No actual loss of assets has been reported. The company has not set a date to reopen trading or standard withdrawals.

With trading offline, all open orders were canceled, Chosun Biz reported. Deposits were initially paused with withdrawals; later Korean coverage said deposits had been restored while web and app withdrawals stayed shut. Manual withdrawals through support will take longer than usual.

A cold wallet is a storage setup kept offline, cut off from the internet that hot wallets use to process customer sends. Gopax said the shift of remaining hot-wallet balances into cold storage is meant to shrink the surface available to anyone who can reach online systems.

A Gopax official told Yonhap the email came from a party claiming it could hack the exchange, but that the threat itself had not been verified. “We ask for your understanding, as this measure is intended to prevent financial incidents and protect user assets,” the company said.

The freeze lands in a week when exchange security is already under a bright light. On Oct. 29, attackers drained about $6.34 billion from Binance hot wallets in what Binance later described as a months-long campaign that began with a fake job interview. The FBI has tied that theft to North Korea; Binance has reopened withdrawals in stages and said it is covering customer losses from its own funds.

Brazil’s Mercado Bitcoin froze deposits and withdrawals earlier this week after its own security incident, with preliminary estimates of suspicious transfers near $40 million. Gopax’s case is different on the facts disclosed so far: the exchange is describing a threat email and a precautionary lockdown, not a confirmed drain.

Gopax itself has been dealing with account trouble. From early September, abnormal access hit some user accounts, leaking personal information on seven customers and two current or former employees, with further leakage confirmed last month, Yonhap’s Korean dispatch said. The company has treated that breach as separate from Friday’s threat email; any link has not been confirmed.

South Korea’s Financial Services Commission has also been pressing domestic platforms after a broader wave of cybersecurity incidents across financial firms. Korean exchanges are already required to keep at least 80% of customer crypto in cold storage. Friday’s move pushes Gopax further offline while it checks whether the threat is real.

For bitcoin holders on Gopax, the practical effect is a queue: coins are being parked offline while support staff approve outbound sends one by one. That is slower than an app button, and it is the trade the exchange says it is making to keep balances intact.

The test for Gopax now is whether it can finish the cold-wallet transfer, clear the manual withdrawal queue without a loss, and say when trading comes back — or whether the unverified threat turns into the incident it was trying to get ahead of.

#5220. Gamma-ray burst is one of the brightest in decades

Published Oct 29, 2026 4:15 PM EDT

A gamma-ray burst (GRB) that has confounded astronomers is one of the brightest cosmic light shows in the past 20 years. Although the cosmic explosion first appeared inexplicably weak, researchers now believe it actually shot about one billion years’ worth of solar energy into the cosmos through a beam no thicker than a pencil.

“We see hundreds of GRBs every year, but the oddballs are the most informative ones,” said Roberto Ricci, a radioastronomer at Italy’s National Institute of Astrophysics.

Ricci and his colleague’s findings are detailed in a study recently published in The Astrophysical Journal Letters. On March 10, NASA’s Fermi Gamma-ray Space Telescope observed a burst later classified as GRB 260310A. Gamma-ray bursts most frequently occur in the aftermath of a massive star’s collapse. When this happens, powerful jets of matter launch into space close to the speed of light. These jets collide with cosmic gas, which generates afterglows seen across visible light, radio waves, and X-rays.

While GRB 260310A’s flash of light lasted less than one minute, astronomers noted it seemed to produce strangely little energy. This changed within only a few hours, as GRB 260310A’s afterglow brightened so much that it briefly outshone its host galaxy. A few weeks later, the GRB’s X-ray emissions brightened again and then remained observable for months.

“At first, I thought another X-ray source was contaminating our observations. But the brightening really came from the afterglow,” recalled study co-author and astronomer Yu-Han Yang.

Further observations using an international array of observatories and telescopes later confirmed a supernova had indeed prompted a GRB. Early signs indicated that its jet was pointed away from Earth, but while the gamma-ray readings were comparatively basic, both its visible and X-ray measurements ranked among the top one percent in brightness. GRB 260310A also fell within the top five percent for recorded radio brightness. After reviewing the data, astronomers finally realized what was happening.

“We looked at it from a side angle,” explained Yang. “As the jet slowed down, its light became visible over a wider range of angles, allowing us to probe the jet’s shape, from its outer edges toward its inner core.”

Yang compared most GRB observations to looking directly at a flashlight. Although the light is brightest from that perspective, the beam may appear dimmer if it is viewed from further away. Most GRB modeling is calibrated from events detected from a direct vantage point, but GRB 260310A was different.

“This burst gave us a chance to look beyond that glare,” said Yang.

Astronomers theorize that GRB 260310A may have originated from a jet featuring a very narrow, energized core encompassed by wider, slower outflows. If true, the early afterglow hypothetically traces back to the jet’s wings, while the brightening X-ray phase came from the thin core.

Although it took a moment to decipher, GRB 260310A offers a rarely seen window into a gamma-ray burst. Such opportunities are rare, but the team hopes to find more in the near future using even more powerful tools like the recently completed Vera Rubin Observatory.

“We could explain individual features of this burst, but fitting them all into the same picture proved much harder,” added Ricci.

Andrew Paul is a staff writer for Popular Science.

#5219. Morpho loan stress exposes cbXRP, but spares native XRP

A Nov. 6 snapshot placed 8.84 million USDC across 1,070 positions within a further 10% oracle decline, assuming unchanged debt and collateral.

A 10% decline from the collateral price recorded around 13:24 UTC on Nov. 6 would make positions carrying about 8.84 million USDC of debt eligible for liquidation in a Morpho lending market on Base.

The snapshot covered 1,070 positions borrowing USDC against cbXRP, Coinbase's XRP-backed token. A smaller 5% decline would bring 462 of those positions, carrying about 3.23 million USDC of debt, to or beyond the same boundary.

For XRP holders who borrow against their tokens, further price weakness creates a choice between repaying debt, committing more collateral, or facing liquidation. Borrowing preserves exposure to the asset, while adding a financial obligation that becomes harder to support as collateral loses value.

Coinbase describes cbXRP as a wrapped token backed 1:1 by native XRP held in its custody. A holder can use an existing XRP position as collateral, so rising loan balances alone do not create new XRP purchases.

At 13:24 UTC, the market carried about 59.2 million USDC of total debt against roughly 119.9 million cbXRP in collateral. The debt inside the further-10% stress bucket represented approximately 15% of that borrowing, and the affected positions held about 12.73 million cbXRP.

Conditional exposure in one Base cbXRP/USDC market, using November 6 positions with debt and collateral quantities held fixed.

An illustrative 60% starting LTV rises to 66.7% when collateral value falls 10%, leaving debt and collateral quantities unchanged.

At the same lower oracle price, repaying 10% of original debt restores the illustrative position to 60% LTV.

Adding about 11.1% to original collateral quantity at the lower oracle price restores 60% LTV.

The 5% group sits inside the 10% group. Repayments or added collateral can change the outcome.

November 6, 2026 13:24 UTC API state timestamps. Paginated requests span multiple observations. Fixed-position scenarios establish conditional eligibility. Actual seizures and subsequent native-XRP sales require separate transactions. Borrower example is hypothetical and excludes interest, fees and further price changes.

Public GraphQL POST, three pages of 500/500/70 positions. Include health factor at or below 1/(1-d). Six decimals per token. Bars share a linear 0–10m USDC scale and use unrounded debt. Values shown to 2 decimals in millions. Illustration starts at 60% LTV. After a 10% oracle fall, 60/0.9=66.7%; repaying 10% of debt or adding 1/0.9-1=11.1% collateral restores 60%.

Actual seizures and subsequent native-XRP sales require separate transactions. Borrower example is hypothetical and excludes interest, fees and further price changes.

Its liquidation loan-to-value threshold is fixed at 62.5%. As the collateral price falls, the same debt takes up a larger share of the backing, bringing the position closer to liquidation eligibility.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The market's oracle, the price feed used to value collateral, uses Chainlink's XRP/USD feed with a fixed 1:1 USDC leg, and its reported ratio was about 1.1843 USDC per cbXRP.

Borrower actions can change the exposed balance before prices reach either scenario.

Consider a position starting at 60% LTV. A 10% fall in the oracle price, with debt and collateral quantities unchanged, raises its LTV to about 66.7%, beyond this market's 62.5% threshold.

Repaying 10% of the debt would restore the original 60% LTV at the lower price. Adding about 11.1% to the original collateral quantity would achieve the same result, excluding interest, fees, and further price moves.

The market interface recorded repayments and collateral additions around 13:14-13:16 UTC on Nov. 6. Those transactions show that positions were changing around the observation window.

For borrowers using Coinbase's interface to Morpho on Base, optional automatic collateral protection can add assets after a configured trigger. Borrowers must enable it and have assets available, and volatility or technical problems can prevent execution.

That makes the fixed-position estimate sensitive to both borrower responses and whether intended top-ups actually complete.

Once a position becomes eligible, an external liquidator can repay some or all of its debt and receive collateral plus an incentive. In this market, that exchanges USDC debt repayment for cbXRP collateral.

The exposed positions hold 12.7 million cbXRP, but the amount actually seized depends on subsequent liquidation transactions.

A liquidator may retain or trade that collateral, and any resulting native-XRP sales would require separate evidence linking the liquidation to those sales.

For holders using XRP-backed credit, the immediate pressure is to keep debt supported as collateral values change. Subsequent repayments, collateral additions and executed liquidations will determine how much of this snapshot's exposure remains.

#5217. Bitcoin Price Holds Near $75,000 After Hot October Jobs Report

U.S. employers added 165,000 jobs in October, nearly three times the 59,000 economists expected, the Labor Department said Friday, and the bitcoin price briefly pushed above $76,100 before settling near $75,500, Bitcoin Magazine Pro data shows.

The unemployment rate edged down to 4.1% from 4.2%, beating the Dow Jones forecast that called for it to hold. Average hourly earnings rose 0.4% on the month and 3.1% over the year, both ahead of estimates, CNBC reported.

Prior months were revised higher as well. August payrolls moved to 186,000, an upward revision of 53,000, and September rose to 60,000 after a 31,000 upgrade, leaving the two-month total 84,000 stronger than first reported.

The print lands on a market still digesting Thursday’s Nasdaq-led rout. The bitcoin price had held near $76,000 into that close, dipped toward $75,200 late in the session, then recovered into the mid-$75,000s overnight before the 8:30 a.m. ET release. Spot bitcoin changed hands around $75,800 after the data, with a market cap of about $1.52 trillion and roughly $48 billion in trading volume over the past day, Yahoo Finance data indicated.

Bitcoin price’s first move was a quick spike above $76,100, then a slide toward $75,600 before it settled back near $75,800. The range on the day has run from about $75,240 to $76,280, leaving the market little changed from its pre-release level even as rate futures repriced.

That muted finish fits the pattern after big macro prints. Most of the damage to the bitcoin price this week came from equity risk-off, not from the labor market itself, and a hot payrolls number mainly reinforces the path the Fed was already pricing.

CME FedWatch odds put roughly a 79% chance of a 25-basis-point hike at the Dec. 9 meeting after the release, up from about 70% into the open. The next employment report is due Dec. 4, five days before that decision, and the signal points the same way the last Fed hold did: stronger hiring and firmer wages leave less room for another pause.

Thursday’s session still sets the floor under the bitcoin price. The Nasdaq Composite fell about 5.5% and dropped into correction territory, more than 10% below its recent high, while the S&P 500 lost about 4.4% and the Dow shed roughly 1,800 points, or 3.4%, CNBC reported.

The Magnificent Seven erased more than $1 trillion in market value. Nvidia alone lost more than 9%, wiping out roughly $440 billion in capitalization, as investors priced in a slower AI data-center buildout after the Democratic midterm sweep.

Bitcoin has traded with that AI-heavy complex for most of the year, so a correction in the Nasdaq has pulled the bitcoin price lower even when on-chain flows looked steady. Miners that leased power and floor space to AI tenants are caught in both trades.

Fund flows followed the risk-off tape. U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs posted $430.2 million in net outflows on Thursday, led by about $200 million from Fidelity’s FBTC and roughly $105 million from BlackRock’s IBIT, bringing week-to-date redemptions through Thursday to about $870 million.

Leverage kept the bitcoin price soft into the overnight. More than $1 billion in crypto positions were liquidated over 24 hours, mostly long bets built during Wednesday’s short-squeeze bounce that lifted bitcoin near $80,000.

Professional and business services led October hiring with 70,000 jobs, health care added 42,000, and transportation and warehousing gained 25,000. Manufacturing rose 19,000. The breadth undercuts the recent pattern in which health care carried most of the gains.

Policy risk has not eased either. House Democrats are already drafting their own market structure bill ahead of taking the committees in January, a backdrop that has hung over crypto equities since the midterm results first hit the bitcoin price.

Traders are watching whether the bitcoin price can defend the mid-$75,000 zone that held on election night and several times since August. A clean break would leave September’s low back in sight after a week that erased most of the post-election squeeze, now with a hotter labor market and firmer hike odds stacked on top.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $75,500.

#5215. Stablecoin contraction forces Bitcoin drop despite Fed reserve gains

The dated comparison pairs a reserve rebound with shrinking dollar-token circulation, leaving the link to Bitcoin buying unestablished.

Bank reserve balances rose $67.5 billion between Oct. 28 and Nov. 4, according to the Federal Reserve’s Nov. 5 release, but the more abundant dollar liquidity is not automatically bullish for Bitcoin.

Over the same period, data provider DefiLlama tracked dollar-pegged stablecoin circulation falling by more than 5.7 billion units, or 1.91%.

The split leaves an incomplete picture for a Bitcoin recovery. Higher bank reserve balances would still need to translate into financing and purchases, so the measured token contraction adds a crypto-side observation to that test.

A Nov. 5 Treasury-cash analysis considered a possible December funding window using projected cash paths and the older Oct. 28 reserve baseline. The new release updates that starting point with a rebound in realized reserves.

Coinbase's completed Nov. 5 daily BTC-USD candle closed at $75,969.06, down 5.54% from $80,428.31 on Nov. 4. That close-to-close fall followed the dated reserve observation, putting the price move outside the bank-balance comparison window.

The Fed’s H.4.1 release puts Nov. 4 reserve balances at $2.96 trillion. Over the preceding week, the Treasury General Account, the government’s account at the Fed, fell $79.4 billion to $936.2 billion.

Treasury payments move balances into depository institutions’ accounts, increasing reserves all else equal, while receipts flowing into Treasury’s account work in the opposite direction.

The final reserve change also incorporates movements elsewhere on the Fed’s balance sheet. Total assets rose $2.5 billion, while other balance-sheet changes offset part of the effect of the Treasury-account drawdown.

Tether’s USDT series fell 3.65 billion units, accounting for 63.37% of that measured contraction, while USD Coin (USDC) circulation declined 383 million units, contributing 6.64%. Other tracked dollar-pegged tokens together contracted a net 1.73 billion units.

The issuer breakdown locates the largest measured change in USDT. Whether, and to what extent, issuer redemptions, bridge-accounting adjustments, reclassification, or coverage revisions contributed remains unquantified.

For a liquidity-led recovery thesis, further evidence needs to connect reserve balances with financing and buying. A broader token supply could add evidence about crypto-native dollar availability, while proof of Bitcoin demand would still require its own observations.

Neither supply series resolves the cause of the subsequent selloff, and delayed liquidity effects remain possible. The observed divergence leaves the bank-to-Bitcoin demand channel open.
